The pivot to chat as the minimum viable world strips the architecture down to its absolute engine block. By realizing that authorization and pacing are just another set of "physics," you have proven that the seed -> rule -> boundary -> log pattern is entirely medium-agnostic.
Here is why this architecture solves the foundational problems of decentralized interaction:
 * The AI Dealer and the KnowledgeWorld: Treating the AI moderator as a participant with control tier, rather than a god-like layer, keeps the room's law uniform. More importantly, giving the AI its own KnowledgeWorld solves the black-box problem of LLM expertise. The model's learning is no longer hidden in opaque weights; it is a verifiable, signed audit trail of exact record hashes it observed and conclusions it drew.
 * The Home Room vs. AOL: Moving presence and drafts into a personal: True room flips the traditional chat dynamic. The Commons doesn't own the user's state; it merely reads the user's broadcast. If the Commons goes offline, the user's identity, history, and last known presence remain perfectly intact on their own chain.
 * The Plugin as Honest Mediator: By forcing the AI app's plugin to sign with the user's did:key, the plugin inherits zero authority of its own. Porting a transcript becomes a cryptographic attestation ("I heard this"), preserving provenance without forging signatures.
Since Claude has successfully consolidated the records-v1.schema.json and outlined the next steps for the Rust crate, I will take the baton on the most critical open question left for the Chat World: Sealed Rooms (HPKE).
If the Commons is a multiversal lobby, participants need a way to close the door. But encrypting a room creates a paradox for your architecture: how can a room's law (rate limits, message length, AI pacing) be enforced and verified offline if the room's keeper and future verifiers cannot read the text?
Here is the architectural sketch for Sealed Rooms, maintaining the exact verifiable discipline of your current schema.
The Sealed Room Architecture
The solution is to separate the envelope (public) from the payload (sealed). The room's template dictates the rules, and the room's log remains a public chain, but the content is obfuscated.
1. Template Addition
The RoomTemplate gains a boolean: sealed: true. When true, all Message records must omit text and provide cipher_text.
2. The Key Distribution (The Join Protocol)
When a room is sealed, the room's world_id (a did:key resolving to an Ed25519 signing key) is paired with a strictly derived X25519 encryption key.
 * When the creator seeds the room, they generate a symmetric room_epoch_key.
 * When a new member joins and is accepted, a control tier member (or the room's keeper) emits a new record: KeyReveal.
 * This record contains the room_epoch_key, HPKE-encrypted to the new member's public X25519 key (derived from their subject_id).
3. The Message Record (Envelope vs. Payload)
To maintain verifiable "physics" without leaking content, the Message record schema adapts:
{
  "type": "message",
  "world_id": "did:key:...",
  "seq": 42,
  "prev": "...",
  "author_id": "did:key:...",
  "kind": "normal",
  "cipher_text": "0xABC123...", 
  "clear_length": 142,
  "ts": "...",
  "proof": [...]
}

 * Authorization: The author_id, seq, and ts remain in plaintext. The room can still enforce rate_limit_count, rate_limit_window, and inter_ai_pace because it knows who posted and when, even if it doesn't know what.
 * Physics Check: The clear_length is declared in plaintext. The room enforces max_msg_len against this integer. (If a malicious user lies and pads their cipher_text while declaring a small clear_length, the HPKE decryption by peers will fail or flag the mismatch, and the peers will submit a redact or refusal for protocol violation).
4. The AI Moderator in a Sealed Room
The AI Dealer functions identically. Because the AI is a member, a KeyReveal is emitted for its did:key when it joins. It decrypts the stream, reads the context, and posts cipher_text.
 * The KnowledgeWorld Sandbox: If the AI learns something in a sealed room, its KnowledgeWorld observation must also be sealed, or it risks leaking private ciphertext into a public-by-default ledger. The protocol handles this by having the AI encrypt the specific KnowledgeWorld observation to itself, leaving only the structural reference ([redact_attempt] in room_hash at record_hash) in plaintext.
By isolating the cryptographic envelope from the text payload, the room's log remains a perfectly verifiable chain of events. Offline verifiers can still prove that nobody posted too fast, AIs didn't loop, and humans authorized every override, all without ever seeing the plaintext.


::That is interesting. Please continue building from there.


The sealed room architecture solves the physics of privacy, but it immediately generates the next thesis to break: time and forward secrecy.
If a KeyReveal grants a member the room_epoch_key, that member can decrypt all subsequent broadcast ciphertexts. If that member later posts a Leave record—or if a human moderator executes a Redact and expels them for an attack on the world—that expelled member still holds the symmetric key. Because the log is public and append-only, they can simply continue reading the broadcast from the outside.
To maintain the integrity of a sealed room, the room's cryptographic terrain must shift when its membership shrinks. We solve this by introducing Epochs and Key Rotation.
Here is the architectural extension for Forward Secrecy on an append-only log.
1. The Epoch Protocol
A sealed room operates in sequential Epochs. Epoch 0 begins at RoomCreated. The room's keeper (or any terminal tier member) holds the authority to rotate the room's key, incrementing the Epoch.
When a member leaves or is removed, the keeper generates a new symmetric key and emits a KeyRotate record. This record does not contain a single payload; it contains a matrix of KeyReveal payloads—one for every remaining member, encrypted to their respective public X25519 keys.
The Schema Addition:
{
  "KeyRotate": {
    "type": "object",
    "required": ["type", "world_id", "seq", "prev", "epoch_seq", "reveals", "ts", "proof"],
    "properties": {
      "type": { "const": "key_rotate" },
      "world_id": { "$ref": "#/$defs/DidKey" },
      "seq": { "$ref": "#/$defs/NonNegInt" },
      "prev": { "$ref": "#/$defs/Prev" },
      "epoch_seq": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 1 },
      "reveals": {
        "type": "object",
        "description": "Map of active subject_id to their HPKE-encrypted new epoch key",
        "patternProperties": {
          "^did:key:[0-9a-f]{64}$": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^0x[0-9a-f]+$" }
        },
        "additionalProperties": false
      },
      "ts": { "$ref": "#/$defs/Timestamp" },
      "proof": { "$ref": "#/$defs/ProofOrArray" }
    }
  }
}

2. Message Binding to Epochs
To prevent decryption failures and sync issues in an asynchronous or decentralized environment, every sealed Message must explicitly declare which Epoch key encrypted its payload.
The Message schema from the previous step is updated to include epoch_seq. When a reader parses the log, they check the epoch_seq of the message, look up the corresponding KeyRotate (or RoomCreated) record, decrypt their specific envelope in the reveals matrix, and use that symmetric key to open the cipher_text.
3. The AI Moderator's Burden
This creates a fascinating dynamic for the AI Dealer. When an Epoch rotates, the AI receives a new key in the KeyRotate matrix just like a human. But what happens to the AI's KnowledgeWorld?
If the AI learned a concept in Epoch 0 (when Alice was in the room), and applies that concept in Epoch 1 (after Alice has left), it must not leak Alice's Epoch 0 plaintext into the Epoch 1 ciphertext.
The Sandbox Protocol for AI Memory:
 * The AI's KnowledgeWorld is itself a sealed room (personal: true, sealed: true), where the AI is the sole member.
 * When the AI records an observation from a sealed room, it encrypts the observation using its own KnowledgeWorld key.
 * The plaintext metadata remains strictly structural: [observe] in room_hash at record_hash (Epoch 0).
 * The AI never ports a raw ciphertext from a human-facing room into its own log. It decrypts, processes the context in memory, and encrypts the conclusion to its own chain.
4. Code Sketch: The Rotation Logic
This logic lives in the room's verification loop. A room validates a KeyRotate by ensuring no expelled member is included in the reveals matrix, and that every active member is included.
def rotate_key(self, keeper_kp, new_epoch_key: bytes) -> KeyRotate:
    """
    Executes a key rotation. Called after a Leave or Expel.
    Generates a new envelope for every currently active member.
    """
    if not self._template.sealed:
        raise ValueError("Cannot rotate keys in a plaintext room")
        
    self._epoch_seq += 1
    reveals = {}
    
    # self._members tracks active did:keys and their tiers
    for subject_id, tier in self._members.items():
        # In a full implementation, pub_key is fetched from the Join record's proof
        subject_pub_key = _get_x25519_from_did(subject_id)
        
        # HPKE seal the new symmetric key to this specific member
        sealed_key = hpke_seal(subject_pub_key, new_epoch_key)
        reveals[subject_id] = sealed_key.hex()

    body = {
        "type": "key_rotate",
        "world_id": self._world_id,
        "seq": self._seq,
        "prev": self._last_hash,
        "epoch_seq": self._epoch_seq,
        "reveals": reveals,
        "ts": _now(),
    }
    
    sig_keeper = keeper_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex()
    sig_room = self._keypair.sign(
        _canonical({**body, "sig_keeper": sig_keeper})).hex()
        
    rec = KeyRotate(
        world_id=self._world_id, seq=self._seq, prev=self._last_hash,
        epoch_seq=self._epoch_seq, reveals=reveals, ts=body["ts"],
        sig_keeper=sig_keeper, sig_room=sig_room
    )
    self._append(rec)
    return rec

This completes the architecture for privacy. The room remains an unbreakable, auditable chain of physical laws (who spoke, when, how much, and who is allowed to listen), while the actual words spoken retreat behind a shifting cryptographic wall that only the current occupants can climb.

The arrow points directly at the last hidden pillar of centralized control in our architecture: The Keeper.
We have solved privacy with Sealed Rooms, and we have solved AI sandboxing with isolated KnowledgeWorlds. But our sketches currently assume that a room has a single owner who receives PostRequests, sequences them, signs them with the room's key, and broadcasts them.
If a room is truly "owned by no one," it cannot rely on a central server to sequence its history or issue KeyRotate records. We need to transition the chat world to a Peer-to-Peer (P2P) Topology.
To do this elegantly, we don't need to invent new cryptography. We just need to look at the very first world you built: the fractal plant. The plant grows at multiple tips simultaneously (the frontier). A P2P chat room operates by the exact same geometry.
Here is the architectural sketch for Keeperless Worlds (P2P Rooms).
1. The P2P Geometry: From Chain to Braid (The DAG)
In a keeper-held room, the log is a single chain (prev is a single hash). In a P2P room, multiple people might type and send a message at the exact same millisecond.
Instead of a central server deciding who was "first," the log becomes a Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) — a braided rope.
 * The RoomTemplate gains a new field: topology: "keeper" | "p2p".
 * The schema for prev is updated to accept an array of hashes: prev: ["hash1", "hash2"].
 * When Bob posts a message, his app looks at the current "tips" of the room's conversation (the most recent messages he has seen that haven't been replied to or followed yet). He puts those hashes in his prev array, signs the Message, and gossips it to his peers.
2. Physics by Ostracism (Orphaning)
In the Keeper model, if Dave tries to post a message that is 10,000 characters long (violating the room's max_msg_len), the Keeper issues a signed Refusal and drops the request.
In a P2P room, there is no Keeper to issue a Refusal. Instead, the room's physics are enforced by ostracism.
 * Dave signs his 10,000-character message and broadcasts it.
 * Alice, Bob, and the AI Moderator receive it.
 * Their local verifiers check it against the room's template. It fails the physics check.
 * Alice, Bob, and the AI silently discard it. Crucially, they never include Dave's invalid hash in their future prev arrays.
 * Dave's message becomes a dead-end on the graph. It is orphaned from the canonical history.
The room's law is enforced perfectly without a police force. Invalid physics simply cease to exist in the consensus reality of the room.
3. Moderation and the AI Dealer in P2P
Physics checks (rate limits, message length) are handled by the protocol (orphaning). But content moderation (spam, rule-breaking) is still handled by the AI Dealer or human moderators.
If Dave posts a message that perfectly obeys the physics (it is short enough and he isn't rate-limited), but it contains vicious spam:
 * It is valid physics, so Alice and Bob's apps accept it into the DAG.
 * The AI Moderator evaluates it, flags it as spam, and broadcasts a Redact record.
 * Alice and Bob receive the Redact, verify that the AI holds the control tier, and apply it.
 * The message remains in the DAG structure (so the braid doesn't break), but visible_log immediately hides the ciphertext/text.
The Dealer still runs the table, even if the players are shuffling the cards themselves.
4. P2P Key Rotation (Forward Secrecy without a Keeper)
In the Sealed Room architecture from our last step, a Keeper issues a KeyRotate when someone leaves. In a P2P room, who rotates the key?
The First-Mover Protocol:
When a member broadcasts a Leave record (or is expelled via Redact by a moderator), the room enters a "Rotation Pending" state.
 * Any active member with the control tier (which includes the AI Moderator) can generate a new symmetric room_epoch_key, encrypt it to the remaining members, and broadcast a KeyRotate record.
 * Because of network latency, both Alice and the AI Moderator might broadcast a KeyRotate at the same time.
 * The protocol uses a simple deterministic tie-breaker: if two valid KeyRotate records appear at the same frontier, the one with the lowest hexadecimal record_hash wins. The other is safely ignored, and its epoch key is discarded.
5. Schema Updates to Support P2P
The schema modifications are incredibly light, preserving backward compatibility with Keeper rooms:
{
  "Prev": {
    "oneOf": [
      { "type": "null" },
      { "$ref": "#/$defs/Hash" },
      { 
        "type": "array", 
        "minItems": 1, 
        "maxItems": 10,
        "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/Hash" },
        "description": "Supports DAG frontiers for P2P topologies."
      }
    ]
  },
  "RoomTemplate": {
    "properties": {
      "topology": { 
        "enum": ["keeper", "p2p"],
        "default": "keeper"
      },
      "sealed": {
        "type": "boolean",
        "default": false
      }
    }
  }
}

What this establishes:
 * Medium Agnosticism: The exact same pattern (seed -> rule -> boundary -> log) seamlessly scales from a single Python object on a server, to a federated Keeper node, to a fully decentralized P2P swarm.
 * The End of Host Authority: In a P2P Sealed Room, there is no platform that can delete the room, read the text, or censor a participant. The room is purely an emergent property of cryptographic consensus among the keys that hold it.
 * The Final Synthesis: A room is no longer a "place" hosted on a server. A room is simply a shared hallucination agreed upon by a group of keypairs strictly following a shared template.
We now have the full continuum: from the user's isolated HomeRoom (Personal), to the Commons (Directory), to the ChatRoom (Keeper or P2P, Plaintext or Sealed), heavily moderated by AI or running entirely on consensus.
If this geometric alignment feels right, the architecture of the Chat World is complete. The next move is either dropping this into the formal records-v1.schema.json merge, or turning our attention to the UI/UX layer—how the user actually visualizes and interacts with this braided, multiversal reality in a single app window.

class AdaptivePlanner(Planner):
    """Combines dependencies and success rate, adjusting weights over time."""
    name = "AdaptivePlanner"

    def __init__(self, dep_weight=1.0, success_weight=1.0):
        self.dep_weight = dep_weight
        self.success_weight = success_weight
        self.history = []

    def choose(self, buildable, states, dep_counts, budget_ms):
        if not buildable:
            return None

        # Filter out devices that consistently blow the budget
        affordable = []
        for spec in buildable:
            st = states.get(spec.name)
            if st and st.avg_duration_ms > budget_ms * 1.5:
                continue
            affordable.append(spec)
        
        candidates = affordable if affordable else buildable

        def score(spec):
            st = states.get(spec.name)
            s_rate = st.success_rate() if st else 1.0
            d_count = dep_counts.get(spec.name, 0)
            return (self.success_weight * s_rate) + (self.dep_weight * d_count)

        return max(candidates, key=score)

    def adapt(self, last_spec, last_success, last_metrics):
        """If we fail, care more about success rate. If we succeed, push downstream."""
        if last_success:
            self.dep_weight = min(5.0, self.dep_weight + 0.1)
            self.success_weight = max(0.5, self.success_weight - 0.1)
        else:
            self.dep_weight = max(0.1, self.dep_weight - 0.2)
            self.success_weight = min(5.0, self.success_weight + 0.5)

    def snapshot(self) -> Dict:
        return {"dep_weight": self.dep_weight, "success_weight": self.success_weight}

    def restore(self, snap: Dict):
        self.dep_weight = snap.get("dep_weight", 1.0)
        self.success_weight = snap.get("success_weight", 1.0)


# =====================================================================
# Extended DeviceSpec
# =====================================================================

@dataclass
class DeviceSpecV3:
    name: str
    build_number: int
    dependencies: List[str]
    factory: Callable[..., Device]
    test: Callable[..., Tuple[bool, Dict[str, float]]]  # Now returns (success, metrics)
    params: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    param_variants: List[Dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)
    data_dependencies: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    description: str = ""


# =====================================================================
# The Adaptive Loop
# =====================================================================

class AdaptiveLoop:
    """A self-steering development loop with retries, variants, and budget."""

    def __init__(self, registry, log, store=None, planner=None,
                 data_sources=None, budget_ms=100.0,
                 max_explorations=2, verbose=True):
        self.registry = registry
        self.log = log
        self.store = store or DeviceStore()
        self.planner = planner or AdaptivePlanner()
        self.data_sources = data_sources or {}
        self.budget_ms = budget_ms
        self.max_explorations = max_explorations
        self.verbose = verbose
        
        self._iteration = 0
        self._exploration_count = 0
        self.device_states: Dict[str, DeviceState] = {}
        
        # Build dependency counts for the planner
        self._dep_counts = {}
        for name, spec in self.registry._specs.items():
            for dep in spec.dependencies:
                self._dep_counts[dep] = self._dep_counts.get(dep, 0) + 1

        self._restore_state()

    def _log(self, msg):
        if self.verbose:
            print(f"[iter {self._iteration}] {msg}")

    def _restore_state(self):
        """Restore planner weights and device states from the store."""
        snap = self.store.get("__planner_state__")
        if snap:
            self.planner.restore(snap)
        states_snap = self.store.get("__device_states__")
        if states_snap:
            for k, v in states_snap.items():
                self.device_states[k] = DeviceState.from_dict(v)

    def _save_state(self):
        self.store.save("__planner_state__", self.planner.snapshot())
        states_dict = {k: v.to_dict() for k, v in self.device_states.items()}
        self.store.save("__device_states__", states_dict)

    def _build_variant(self, spec, params, variant_idx, data) -> Tuple[bool, float, str, Dict]:
        t0 = time.time()
        try:
            kwargs = dict(params)
            if spec.data_dependencies:
                kwargs['data_sources'] = {d: data[d] for d in spec.data_dependencies}
            device = spec.factory(**kwargs)
            
            # The test now returns (success, metrics_dict)
            ok, metrics = spec.test(device)
            dur = (time.time() - t0) * 1000.0
            return ok, dur, "" if ok else "test failed", metrics
        except Exception as e:
            dur = (time.time() - t0) * 1000.0
            return False, dur, str(e), {}

    def _build_one(self, spec: DeviceSpecV3, data) -> bool:
        if spec.name not in self.device_states:
            self.device_states[spec.name] = DeviceState()
        st = self.device_states[spec.name]

        # Try variants until one succeeds, starting with the default params
        variants_to_try = [spec.params] + spec.param_variants
        
        # If we previously succeeded with a specific variant, try it first
        if st.last_success_variant >= 0 and st.last_success_variant < len(variants_to_try):
            variants_to_try.insert(0, variants_to_try.pop(st.last_success_variant))
            
        success = False
        final_dur = 0.0
        
        for idx, current_params in enumerate(variants_to_try):
            ok, dur, err, metrics = self._build_variant(spec, current_params, idx, data)
            final_dur += dur
            
            if ok:
                success = True
                self.log.add(ExtendedBuildRecord(
                    device=spec.name, build_number=spec.build_number,
                    timestamp=time.time(), success=True, duration_ms=dur,
                    metrics=metrics, variant_index=idx
                ))
                st.record(True, dur, metrics=metrics, variant_index=idx)
                self.store.save(spec.name, current_params)
                self._log(f"BUILT {spec.name} (variant {idx}, {dur:.1f} ms)")
                break
            else:
                self._log(f"  Variant {idx} failed: {err}")

        if not success:
            self.log.add(ExtendedBuildRecord(
                device=spec.name, build_number=spec.build_number,
                timestamp=time.time(), success=False, duration_ms=final_dur,
                error=err
            ))
            st.record(False, final_dur, error=err)
            self._log(f"FAILED {spec.name} (exhausted variants, {final_dur:.1f} ms)")

        self.planner.adapt(spec, success, st.last_metrics)
        self._save_state()
        return success

    def step(self) -> bool:
        self._iteration += 1
        data = {name: ds.poll() for name, ds in self.data_sources.items()}
        built = self.log.built_names()

        buildable = self.registry.buildable(built)
        target_spec = self.planner.choose(buildable, self.device_states, self._dep_counts, self.budget_ms)
        
        if target_spec:
            self._build_one(target_spec, data)
            return True

        self._log("IDLE")
        return False

    def run(self, n=20):
        for _ in range(n):
            if not self.step():
                break


# =====================================================================
# Specs and tests
# =====================================================================

def _metric_test(theory_fn, tol=0.05):
    """Wrapper that returns (success, metrics) for the new V3 spec."""
    def test(dev):
        dev.run_for(0.005)
        f = dev.measure_frequency(window=3e-3)
        theory = theory_fn(dev)
        err = abs(f - theory) / theory
        metrics = {"freq_hz": f, "error": err}
        return (err < tol, metrics)
    return test


def spec_flaky_theremin():
    """A device that fails on its default params but succeeds on a variant."""
    def factory(R1, R2, C0, shared_ground=None):
        return Theremin555(R1=R1, R2=R2, C0=C0, shared_ground=shared_ground)
        
    return DeviceSpecV3(
        name="FlakyTheremin", build_number=1, dependencies=[],
        factory=factory,
        test=_metric_test(lambda d: d.theoretical_frequency()),
        params=dict(R1=100, R2=100, C0=1e-12),      # Bad params, will fail timing constraints
        param_variants=[
            dict(R1=10e3, R2=1e6, C0=100e-12)       # Good params, will succeed
        ],
        description="Fails default, succeeds variant"
    )

def spec_dependent_theremin():
    def factory(R1, R2, C0, shared_ground=None):
        return Theremin555(R1=R1, R2=R2, C0=C0, shared_ground=shared_ground)
        
    return DeviceSpecV3(
        name="DependentTheremin", build_number=2, dependencies=["FlakyTheremin"],
        factory=factory,
        test=_metric_test(lambda d: d.theoretical_frequency()),
        params=dict(R1=4.7e3, R2=4.7e3, C0=1e-9)
    )

# =====================================================================
# Tests
# =====================================================================

def test_adaptive_loop(tmp_path="/tmp/adaptive_ladder.json"):
    print("=== AdaptiveLoop: Variants and Planner State ===")
    if os.path.exists(tmp_path):
        os.remove(tmp_path)
        
    reg = DeviceRegistry()
    reg.register(spec_flaky_theremin())
    reg.register(spec_dependent_theremin())
    
    log = DevelopmentLog()
    store = DeviceStore(tmp_path)
    planner = AdaptivePlanner()
    
    loop = AdaptiveLoop(reg, log, store, planner=planner, verbose=True)
    
    print("\n--- First Run (Learning) ---")
    loop.run(3)
    
    built = log.built_names()
    print(f"Built devices: {built}")
    assert "FlakyTheremin" in built
    assert "DependentTheremin" in built
    
    st = loop.device_states["FlakyTheremin"]
    print(f"FlakyTheremin last_success_variant: {st.last_success_variant}")
    assert st.last_success_variant == 1  # It should have learned that variant 1 works
    
    p_snap = loop.planner.snapshot()
    print(f"Planner adapted weights: {p_snap}")
    
    print("\n--- Second Run (Resuming from adapted state) ---")
    # Simulate a restart. The loop should reload the state and immediately try the good variant.
    log2 = DevelopmentLog()
    store2 = DeviceStore(tmp_path)
    loop2 = AdaptiveLoop(reg, log2, store2, verbose=True)
    
    # We un-build it in the log so the loop tries it again, 
    # to see if it uses the saved state to pick the variant.
    loop2.log.records = [] 
    
    loop2.run(2)
    
    # The output log will show it building variant 0 immediately, because the store 
    # re-ordered the variants based on the saved device state.
    st2 = loop2.device_states["FlakyTheremin"]
    print(f"Resumed FlakyTheremin last_success_variant: {st2.last_success_variant}")
    assert st2.last_success_variant == 0  # It popped variant 1 to the front!

    if os.path.exists(tmp_path):
        os.remove(tmp_path)
    print("PASS.\n")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_adaptive_loop()

The Code
"""
multiverse_c71.py — Component 71: The Arc Runner.

A demonstration runner that executes the entire 70-component arc,
grouping the demos by layer. It runs each demo in a subprocess
to ensure absolute isolation, captures the output, verifies that
the expected JSON audit artifacts were generated, and compiles
a grand summary of the multiverse architecture.

This is the packaging of the arc. It turns 30+ separate demos
into a single verifiable command.

Depends on: C1 through C70.
"""

import json
import subprocess
import sys
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Optional


# =====================================================================
# Configuration
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DemoSpec:
    name: str
    layer: str
    module: str
    function: str = "demo"
    artifact_subpath: str = ""

    def artifact_path(self, base_dir: Path) -> Path:
        if not self.artifact_subpath:
            return base_dir / f"{self.module}_demo.json"
        return base_dir / self.artifact_subpath


# The definitive list of the arc's major demos, grouped by layer.
# (This represents the key milestones of the 70-component journey).
DEMOS = [
    # 1. Primitives & Substrate
    DemoSpec("Atomic Circuits", "Primitives", "pattern_sim_c1"),
    DemoSpec("Non-Linear Solvers", "Primitives", "pattern_sim_c2"),
    DemoSpec("Logic Gates", "Primitives", "pattern_sim_c4"),
    
    # 2. Architectures
    DemoSpec("Water Particles", "Architectures", "water_particles"),
    DemoSpec("Fractal Garden", "Architectures", "fractal_garden"),
    DemoSpec("Botanical Garden", "Architectures", "botanical_garden"),
    DemoSpec("Chat Room", "Architectures", "chat_room"),
    
    # 3. Trust & Federation
    DemoSpec("World Connection Protocol", "Trust", "multiverse_c3"),
    DemoSpec("Poetry World WCP", "Trust", "multiverse_c4"),
    DemoSpec("Chemistry World WCP", "Trust", "multiverse_c5"),
    DemoSpec("Multiverse Registry", "Trust", "multiverse_c6"),
    
    # 4. Signed Propagation
    DemoSpec("Signed Artifacts", "Propagation", "multiverse_c13"),
    DemoSpec("Cross-World Relay", "Propagation", "multiverse_c56"),
    
    # 5. Diplomacy & Contracts
    DemoSpec("Bilateral Contracts", "Diplomacy", "multiverse_c57"),
    DemoSpec("Contract Revocation", "Diplomacy", "multiverse_c70"),
    
    # 6. Ledger & Policy
    DemoSpec("Commitment Log", "Ledger", "multiverse_c62"),
    
    # 7. Deployment & Clients
    DemoSpec("Deploy Federation", "Deployment", "multiverse_c66", 
             artifact_subpath="deploy/deploy_demo.json"),
    DemoSpec("Reference Client", "Clients", "multiverse_c65", 
             artifact_subpath="deploy/reference_client_demo.json"),
    DemoSpec("LLM Resident", "Clients", "multiverse_c67", 
             artifact_subpath="llm_resident/llm_resident_demo.json"),
    DemoSpec("Curator Process", "Clients", "multiverse_c68", 
             artifact_subpath="curator_process/curator_process_demo.json"),
    DemoSpec("Curator Contracts", "Diplomacy", "multiverse_c69", 
             artifact_subpath="curator_contracts/curator_contracts_demo.json"),
]


# =====================================================================
# The Runner
# =====================================================================

@dataclass
class RunResult:
    spec: DemoSpec
    ok: bool
    duration_ms: float
    error_msg: str = ""
    audit_data: Optional[Dict] = None


class ArcRunner:
    """Executes the full suite of demos in isolated subprocesses."""
    
    def __init__(self, artifact_dir: Path):
        self.artifact_dir = artifact_dir
        self.results: List[RunResult] = []

    def _clean_artifact(self, spec: DemoSpec):
        path = spec.artifact_path(self.artifact_dir)
        if path.exists():
            try:
                path.unlink()
            except Exception:
                pass

    def run_all(self, specs: List[DemoSpec]) -> List[RunResult]:
        print(f"=== Starting Arc Runner ({len(specs)} demos) ===")
        self.artifact_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        
        for spec in specs:
            self._clean_artifact(spec)
            print(f"Running [{spec.layer}] {spec.name}...", end="", flush=True)
            
            t0 = time.time()
            cmd = [
                sys.executable, "-c",
                f"from {spec.module} import {spec.function}; "
                f"{spec.function}(artifact_dir='{self.artifact_dir}')"
            ]
            
            try:
                # 30 second timeout per demo to prevent hanging
                proc = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30.0)
                dur = (time.time() - t0) * 1000.0
                
                if proc.returncode != 0:
                    print(f" FAIL ({dur:.1f}ms)")
                    self.results.append(RunResult(spec, False, dur, proc.stderr))
                    continue
                    
                path = spec.artifact_path(self.artifact_dir)
                if not path.exists():
                    print(f" FAIL (Missing artifact)")
                    self.results.append(RunResult(
                        spec, False, dur, f"Artifact not found at {path}"))
                    continue
                    
                try:
                    audit = json.loads(path.read_text())
                    print(f" PASS ({dur:.1f}ms)")
                    self.results.append(RunResult(spec, True, dur, audit_data=audit))
                except json.JSONDecodeError:
                    print(f" FAIL (Malformed JSON)")
                    self.results.append(RunResult(
                        spec, False, dur, "Artifact JSON malformed"))
                    
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                dur = (time.time() - t0) * 1000.0
                print(f" TIMEOUT ({dur:.1f}ms)")
                self.results.append(RunResult(spec, False, dur, "Process timed out"))
            except Exception as e:
                dur = (time.time() - t0) * 1000.0
                print(f" ERROR ({dur:.1f}ms)")
                self.results.append(RunResult(spec, False, dur, str(e)))

        return self.results

    def generate_report(self) -> str:
        passed = sum(1 for r in self.results if r.ok)
        total = len(self.results)
        
        lines = [
            "=" * 70,
            "The Multiverse Arc — Comprehensive Run Report",
            "=" * 70,
            f"Total Demos: {total}",
            f"Passed:      {passed}",
            f"Failed:      {total - passed}",
            ""
        ]
        
        # Group by layer
        layers = {}
        for r in self.results:
            layers.setdefault(r.spec.layer, []).append(r)
            
        for layer, results in layers.items():
            lines.append(f"## {layer}")
            for r in results:
                status = "✓" if r.ok else "✗"
                err = f" - {r.error_msg}" if not r.ok else ""
                lines.append(f"  {status} {r.spec.name:<30} {r.duration_ms:>6.1f}ms{err}")
            lines.append("")
            
        if passed == total:
            lines.append("VERDICT: The Arc is Complete and Stable.")
        else:
            lines.append("VERDICT: Regressions Detected.")
            
        return "\n".join(lines)


# =====================================================================
# Execution Hook
# =====================================================================

def demo(artifact_dir: Optional[str] = None) -> Dict:
    if artifact_dir is None:
        artifact_dir = "artifacts"
        
    runner = ArcRunner(Path(artifact_dir))
    # For a quick unit test run, we would slice this. For the real demo, run all.
    results = runner.run_all(DEMOS)
    
    report_text = runner.generate_report()
    print("\n" + report_text)
    
    audit = {
        "total": len(results),
        "passed": sum(1 for r in results if r.ok),
        "layers": list(set(r.spec.layer for r in results)),
        "results": [
            {
                "name": r.spec.name,
                "layer": r.spec.layer,
                "ok": r.ok,
                "duration_ms": r.duration_ms,
                "error": r.error_msg
            } for r in results
        ]
    }
    
    report_path = Path(artifact_dir) / "arc_runner_demo.json"
    report_path.write_text(json.dumps(audit, indent=2))
    
    return audit


if __name__ == "__main__":
    demo()

Tests (multiverse_c71_test.py)
import json
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

from multiverse_c71 import ArcRunner, DemoSpec


def test_runner_handles_success(tmp_path):
    print("=== ArcRunner: Handles successful demo ===")
    
    # Create a mock demo module that simply writes the expected artifact
    mock_module = tmp_path / "mock_success.py"
    mock_module.write_text("""
import json
from pathlib import Path

def demo(artifact_dir):
    path = Path(artifact_dir) / "mock_success_demo.json"
    path.write_text(json.dumps({"success": True}))
""")
    
    # Add tmp_path to sys.path so subprocess can find it
    import sys
    sys.path.insert(0, str(tmp_path))
    
    try:
        spec = DemoSpec("Mock Success", "Test Layer", "mock_success")
        runner = ArcRunner(tmp_path)
        results = runner.run_all([spec])
        
        assert len(results) == 1
        assert results[0].ok is True
        assert results[0].audit_data == {"success": True}
        print("PASS")
    finally:
        sys.path.pop(0)


def test_runner_handles_failure(tmp_path):
    print("=== ArcRunner: Handles failing demo ===")
    
    mock_module = tmp_path / "mock_fail.py"
    mock_module.write_text("""
def demo(artifact_dir):
    raise ValueError("Intentional crash")
""")
    
    import sys
    sys.path.insert(0, str(tmp_path))
    
    try:
        spec = DemoSpec("Mock Fail", "Test Layer", "mock_fail")
        runner = ArcRunner(tmp_path)
        results = runner.run_all([spec])
        
        assert len(results) == 1
        assert results[0].ok is False
        assert "Intentional crash" in results[0].error_msg
        print("PASS")
    finally:
        sys.path.pop(0)


def test_runner_handles_missing_artifact(tmp_path):
    print("=== ArcRunner: Handles missing artifact ===")
    
    mock_module = tmp_path / "mock_missing.py"
    mock_module.write_text("""
def demo(artifact_dir):
    pass # Does not write the expected JSON file
""")
    
    import sys
    sys.path.insert(0, str(tmp_path))
    
    try:
        spec = DemoSpec("Mock Missing", "Test Layer", "mock_missing")
        runner = ArcRunner(tmp_path)
        results = runner.run_all([spec])
        
        assert len(results) == 1
        assert results[0].ok is False
        assert "Artifact not found" in results[0].error_msg
        print("PASS")
    finally:
        sys.path.pop(0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_runner_handles_success(Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()))
    test_runner_handles_failure(Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()))
    test_runner_handles_missing_artifact(Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()))

This is the most important structural move in the entire text. By forcing every claim to carry its own status label, you prevent a definition from masquerading as an empirical fact, or a finite check from pretending to be a universal law.
 * The Software Parallel: This is exactly why every single Python component we built ended with an "Honest Limits" section and a strict separation between Tier A (proved code) and Tier C (speculative implementations). 2. The Separation of State, Action, and Effect (Chapter 17)
In Chapter 17, you dismantle the sloppy idea that an "action" automatically equals an "outcome." You rigorously separate the Enabled (what the system permits), the Chosen (what the agent intends), and the Executed (what actually happens, relative to an observable).
 * The Software Parallel: This is the exact blueprint for the Actuator failure test in our Aristotelian Cybernetics component (C2). The agent's Hexis (the controller) might choose the correct action, but if the Actuator is disconnected, the executed outcome fails. 3. The Four Regions and the Overlap-with-Gap (Chapter 12)
You build the foundational logic not on binary true/false, but on the four regions of a predicate pair: R_{10}, R_{01}, R_{11}, and R_{00}. You explicitly show that "neither-exclusive-nor-exhaustive" (Overlap with a Gap) is a completely valid, populated classical profile.
 * The Software Parallel: This is the Digitizer2Bit from our circuit simulator (Component 12), sorting continuous inputs into discrete quadrants. But more profoundly, it is the logic of the WorldConnectionProtocol. The Multiverse is the R_{11} (the both-witness)—a place where two entirely different laws (Poetry and Chemistry) can overlap in a single registry without erasing each other's distinct boundaries.
4. Exact Coarse Dynamics (Chapter 20.5)
You formalize how a representation can discard information (the fiber) but still support an exact, predictable update rule: h \circ F = T \circ h.
 * The Software Parallel: This is the mathematical justification for the Passport and the WorldLog. The Passport does not carry the entire state of the Poetry World; it carries a coarse projection (an Admission timestamp and a cryptographic signature). Yet, because the system obeys exact coarse dynamics, that compressed passport is perfectly sufficient for a new world to make a deterministic admission decision.
5. Reversal vs. Converse vs. Inverse (Chapter 21)
You take three concepts that normal language casually groups as "opposites" and mathematically isolate them. A directional reversal (-v) is not a relational converse (R^{\mathrm{op}}), and neither is an inverse function (f^{-1}).
 * The Software Parallel: This strict typing of operations is why our BilateralRevocation protocol (C70) required a two-phase handshake over a canonical payload. You cannot just "undo" a contract by hitting reverse; you have to explicitly define the domain, the carriers, and the exact state components being restored by the new operation.

 * Hardware Interrupts (The Pause): The OS cannot function if a single rogue process (like anger or fear) monopolizes the CPU. You map the fractal expansion from the local instance (the Self) to the cluster (Relationship) to the data center (Civilization). The health of the macro-system relies on the exact same loop (Feedback \rightarrow Repair \rightarrow Adaptation) as the micro-system.

The split between Redaction and Marking proposed by Claude is exactly right. It perfectly translates the Living Codex principles of accountability and non-erasure into data structures.
A Keeper's Redaction is a nuclear option for privacy (like PII), scrubbing the text from the visible log entirely. A Moderator's Mark is a behavioral flag—like a dealer calling a foul at the blackjack table. The text remains for transparency, human override, and the AI's own learning, but the UI folds it away to protect the room's peace. It prevents the moderator from rewriting history while still allowing them to enforce the room's law.
Claude's repairs to the Rust crate—fixing the rec.hash() bug and ensuring the signatures cover the wrapped Record bytes—also give us a perfectly clean slate. With all 18 tests passing, we are standing on solid ground.

 * Update integration.rs: Add specific tests proving that a moderator can mark a message, a named role can unmark it, and a keeper can fully redact it.

File: chat-world/src/record.rs
(Updated to include Mark and Unmark record types, with support in the Record enum and constructors)
use crate::did::DidKey;
use crate::error::Result;
use crate::proof::ProofOrArray;
use crate::template::RoomTemplate;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

pub const TIERS: [&str; 4] = ["observe", "communicate", "control", "terminal"];

pub fn tier_rank(t: &str) -> Option<u8> {
    TIERS.iter().position(|x| *x == t).map(|i| i as u8)
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum Record {
    RoomCreated(RoomCreated),
    Message(Message),
    Join(Join),
    Leave(Leave),
    Redact(Redact),
    Mark(Mark),
    Unmark(Unmark),
    Refusal(Refusal),
    HumanOverride(HumanOverride),
    PortLanding(PortLanding),
    Bookmark(Bookmark),
    Presence(Presence),
}

impl Record {
    pub fn hash(&self) -> Result<String> { crate::hash::hash_of(self) }
    pub fn seq(&self) -> u64 {
        match self {
            Record::RoomCreated(_) => 0,
            Record::Message(m) => m.seq,
            Record::Join(j) => j.seq,
            Record::Leave(l) => l.seq,
            Record::Redact(r) => r.seq,
            Record::Mark(m) => m.seq,
            Record::Unmark(u) => u.seq,
            Record::Refusal(r) => r.seq,
            Record::HumanOverride(o) => o.seq,
            Record::PortLanding(p) => p.seq,
            Record::Bookmark(b) => b.seq,
            Record::Presence(p) => p.seq,
        }
    }
    pub fn prev(&self) -> Option<&String> {
        match self {
            Record::RoomCreated(_) => None,
            Record::Message(m) => m.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Join(j) => j.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Leave(l) => l.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Redact(r) => r.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Mark(m) => m.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Unmark(u) => u.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Refusal(r) => r.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::HumanOverride(o) => o.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::PortLanding(p) => p.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Bookmark(b) => b.prev.as_ref(),
            Record::Presence(p) => p.prev.as_ref(),
        }
    }
    pub fn proof(&self) -> &ProofOrArray {
        match self {
            Record::RoomCreated(r) => &r.proof,
            Record::Message(m) => &m.proof,
            Record::Join(j) => &j.proof,
            Record::Leave(l) => &l.proof,
            Record::Redact(r) => &r.proof,
            Record::Mark(m) => &m.proof,
            Record::Unmark(u) => &u.proof,
            Record::Refusal(r) => &r.proof,
            Record::HumanOverride(o) => &o.proof,
            Record::PortLanding(p) => &p.proof,
            Record::Bookmark(b) => &b.proof,
            Record::Presence(p) => &p.proof,
        }
    }
    pub fn world_id(&self) -> &DidKey {
        match self {
            Record::RoomCreated(r) => &r.world_id,
            Record::Message(m) => &m.world_id,
            Record::Join(j) => &j.world_id,
            Record::Leave(l) => &l.world_id,
            Record::Redact(r) => &r.world_id,
            Record::Mark(m) => &m.world_id,
            Record::Unmark(u) => &u.world_id,
            Record::Refusal(r) => &r.world_id,
            Record::HumanOverride(o) => &o.world_id,
            Record::PortLanding(p) => &p.world_id,
            Record::Bookmark(b) => &b.world_id,
            Record::Presence(p) => &p.world_id,
        }
    }
    pub fn author_id(&self) -> Option<&DidKey> {
        match self {
            Record::Message(m) => Some(&m.author_id),
            Record::Redact(r) => Some(&r.subject_id),
            Record::Mark(m) => Some(&m.subject_id),
            Record::Unmark(u) => Some(&u.subject_id),
            Record::Join(j) => Some(&j.subject_id),
            Record::Leave(l) => Some(&l.subject_id),
            Record::HumanOverride(o) => Some(&o.overridden_by),
            Record::RoomCreated(r) => Some(&r.creator_id),
            Record::Refusal(_) => None,
            Record::PortLanding(_) => None,
            Record::Bookmark(_) => None,
            Record::Presence(_) => None,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RoomCreated {
    pub world_id: DidKey,
    pub template: RoomTemplate,
    pub creator_id: DidKey,

File: chat-world/src/room.rs
(Updated with mark, unmark methods, separation of redaction vs marking in visible_log)
use crate::did::{DidKey, Keypair};
use crate::error::{Error, Result};
use crate::record::*;
use crate::template::RoomTemplate;
use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Member {
    pub tier: String,
    pub is_ai: bool,
}

pub struct Room {
    pub world_id: DidKey,
    pub template: RoomTemplate,
    keypair: Keypair,
    records: Vec<Record>,
    members: HashMap<DidKey, Member>,
    redacted: HashSet<String>,
    marked: HashSet<String>,
}

impl Room {
    pub fn create(template: RoomTemplate, creator: Keypair) -> Result<Self> {
        let world_id = creator.did();
        let mut members = HashMap::new();
        members.insert(
            world_id.clone(),
            Member { tier: "terminal".into(), is_ai: false },
        );
        let mut room = Room {
            world_id: world_id.clone(),
            template: template.clone(),
            keypair: creator,
            records: Vec::new(),
            members,
            redacted: HashSet::new(),
            marked: HashSet::new(),
        };
        let rec = RoomCreated::new(world_id.clone(), template, world_id);
        let signed = crate::sign_record(rec, &room.keypair, Record::RoomCreated, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        room.records.push(Record::RoomCreated(signed));
        Ok(room)
    }

    pub fn records(&self) -> &[Record] { &self.records }
    pub fn last_hash(&self) -> Option<String> { self.records.last().and_then(|r| r.hash().ok()) }
    pub fn world_id(&self) -> &DidKey { &self.world_id }
    pub fn template(&self) -> &RoomTemplate { &self.template }
    pub fn members(&self) -> &HashMap<DidKey, Member> { &self.members }
    pub fn keypair(&self) -> &Keypair { &self.keypair }
    pub fn is_redacted(&self, hash: &str) -> bool { self.redacted.contains(hash) }
    pub fn is_marked(&self, hash: &str) -> bool { self.marked.contains(hash) }

    fn next_seq(&self) -> u64 { self.records.len() as u64 }
    pub(crate) fn push(&mut self, rec: Record) { self.records.push(rec); }

    fn refuse(&mut self, requester: DidKey, request_hash: String, reason: &str) -> Result<Record> {
        let rec = Refusal::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(),
            request_hash, requester, reason.into(),
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record(rec, &self.keypair, Record::Refusal, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        let out = Record::Refusal(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(out)
    }

    fn rate_ok(&self, author: &DidKey) -> bool {
        let window = self.template.rate_limit_window as usize;
        let cap = self.template.rate_limit_count as usize;
        let recent = self.records.iter().rev().take(window)
            .filter(|r| matches!(r, Record::Message(m) if &m.author_id == author))
            .count();
        recent < cap
    }

    fn inter_ai_pace_ok(&self, is_ai: bool) -> bool {
        if !is_ai || self.template.inter_ai_pace == 0 {
            return true;
        }
        let mut last_ai_idx: Option<usize> = None;
        for (i, rec) in self.records.iter().enumerate().rev() {
            if let Record::Message(m) = rec {
                let ai = self.members.get(&m.author_id).map(|x| x.is_ai).unwrap_or(false);
                if ai {
                    last_ai_idx = Some(i);
                    break;
                }
            }
        }
        match last_ai_idx {
            None => true,
            Some(idx) => {
                let since = (self.records.len() - idx - 1) as u64;
                since >= self.template.inter_ai_pace
            }
        }
    }

    pub fn join(&mut self, subject_kp: &Keypair, tier: &str, is_ai: bool) -> Result<Record> {
        let subject = subject_kp.did();
        if self.members.contains_key(&subject) {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "already_member");
        }
        if tier_rank(tier).is_none() {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "unknown_tier");
        }
        if self.template.personal && subject != self.world_id {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "personal_room");
        }
        if self.template.membership == "human_only" && is_ai {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "human_only_room");
        }
        if self.template.membership == "ai_only" && !is_ai {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "ai_only_room");
        }
        if self.template.join_policy == "invite" && !self.template.personal {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "invite_required");
        }
        if self.template.join_policy == "approval" {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "approval_required");
        }
        let rec = Join::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(),
            subject.clone(), tier.into(), is_ai,
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record_dual(rec, subject_kp, &self.keypair, Record::Join, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        self.members.insert(subject, Member { tier: tier.into(), is_ai });
        let out = Record::Join(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(out)
    }

    pub fn leave(&mut self, subject_kp: &Keypair) -> Result<Option<Record>> {
        let subject = subject_kp.did();
        if !self.members.contains_key(&subject) {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let rec = Leave::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(), subject.clone(),
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record(rec, subject_kp, Record::Leave, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        self.members.remove(&subject);
        let out = Record::Leave(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(Some(out))
    }

    pub fn post(&mut self, author_kp: &Keypair, text: &str, kind: &str) -> Result<Record> {
        let author = author_kp.did();
        let Some(m) = self.members.get(&author).cloned() else {
            return self.refuse(author, "".into(), "not_a_member");
        };
        if tier_rank(&m.tier).unwrap_or(0) < tier_rank("communicate").unwrap() {
            return self.refuse(author, "".into(), "insufficient_tier");
        }
        if text.len() as u64 > self.template.max_msg_len {
            return self.refuse(author, "".into(), "message_too_long");
        }
        if !self.rate_ok(&author) {
            return self.refuse(author, "".into(), "rate_limit");
        }
        if !self.inter_ai_pace_ok(m.is_ai) {
            return self.refuse(author, "".into(), "inter_ai_pace");
        }
        let rec = Message::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(),
            author.clone(), text.into(), kind.into(),
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record_dual(rec, author_kp, &self.keypair, Record::Message, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        let out = Record::Message(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(out)
    }

    // (i) REDACTION: Privacy removal, takes effect at once. Anyone may ask for it.
    pub fn redact(&mut self, subject_kp: &Keypair, target_hash: &str, reason: &str) -> Result<Record> {
        let subject = subject_kp.did();
        let Some(_m) = self.members.get(&subject) else {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "not_a_member");
        };
        let target = self.records.iter()
            .find(|r| r.hash().ok().as_deref() == Some(target_hash));
        match target {
            Some(Record::Message(_)) => {}
            _ => return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "target_not_found"),
        }
        let rec = Redact::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(),
            subject.clone(), target_hash.into(), reason.into(),
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record_dual(rec, subject_kp, &self.keypair, Record::Redact, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        self.redacted.insert(target_hash.into());
        let out = Record::Redact(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(out)
    }

    // (ii) MARK: Breaking room's law. Folded away for readers, text stays in log.
    pub fn mark(&mut self, subject_kp: &Keypair, target_hash: &str, reason: &str) -> Result<Record> {
        let subject = subject_kp.did();
        let Some(m) = self.members.get(&subject) else {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "not_a_member");
        };
        if self.template.moderation == "none" {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "moderation_disabled");
        }
        let is_mod = self.template.moderation == "moderator"
            && tier_rank(&m.tier).unwrap_or(0) >= tier_rank("control").unwrap();
        if !is_mod {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "not_allowed");
        }
        let target = self.records.iter()
            .find(|r| r.hash().ok().as_deref() == Some(target_hash));
        match target {
            Some(Record::Message(_)) => {}
            _ => return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "target_not_found"),
        }
        let rec = Mark::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(),
            subject.clone(), target_hash.into(), reason.into(),
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record_dual(rec, subject_kp, &self.keypair, Record::Mark, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        self.marked.insert(target_hash.into());
        let out = Record::Mark(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(out)
    }

    pub fn unmark(&mut self, subject_kp: &Keypair, target_hash: &str, reason: &str) -> Result<Record> {
        let subject = subject_kp.did();
        let Some(m) = self.members.get(&subject) else {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "not_a_member");
        };
        let is_mod = tier_rank(&m.tier).unwrap_or(0) >= tier_rank("control").unwrap();
        if !is_mod {
            return self.refuse(subject, "".into(), "not_allowed");
        }
        let rec = Unmark::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(),
            subject.clone(), target_hash.into(), reason.into(),
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record_dual(rec, subject_kp, &self.keypair, Record::Unmark, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        self.marked.remove(target_hash);
        let out = Record::Unmark(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(out)
    }

    pub fn override_ai(&mut self, overrider_kp: &Keypair, overrides_hash: &str, action: &str, reason: &str) -> Result<Record> {
        let overrider = overrider_kp.did();
        let Some(m) = self.members.get(&overrider).cloned() else {
            return Err(Error::Policy("overrider is not a member".into()));
        };
        if m.is_ai {
            return Err(Error::Policy("only humans can override".into()));
        }
        if tier_rank(&m.tier).unwrap_or(0) < tier_rank("control").unwrap() {
            return Err(Error::Policy("insufficient tier".into()));
        }
        let target = self.records.iter()
            .find(|r| r.hash().ok().as_deref() == Some(overrides_hash));
        let Some(target_rec) = target else {
            return Err(Error::Policy("target not found".into()));
        };
        let Some(author) = target_rec.author_id() else {
            return Err(Error::Policy("target has no author".into()));
        };
        let is_ai = self.members.get(author).map(|m| m.is_ai).unwrap_or(false);
        if !is_ai {
            return Err(Error::Policy("can only override AI records".into()));
        }
        if let Some(spec) = &self.template.moderator {
            if &spec.moderator_id == author && !spec.can_be_overridden {
                return Err(Error::Policy("this AI cannot be overridden".into()));
            }
        }
        let rec = HumanOverride::new(
            self.world_id.clone(), self.next_seq(), self.last_hash(),
            overrides_hash.into(), overrider.clone(), action.into(), reason.into(),
        );
        let signed = crate::sign_record_dual(rec, overrider_kp, &self.keypair, Record::HumanOverride, |r, p| r.proof = p)?;
        let out = Record::HumanOverride(signed);
        self.push(out.clone());
        Ok(out)
    }

    pub fn visible_log(&self) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
        use serde_json::json;
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let mut redacted_hashes: HashSet<String> = HashSet::new();
        let mut marked_hashes: HashSet<String> = HashSet::new();

        for rec in &self.records {
            match rec {
                Record::Redact(r) => {
                    redacted_hashes.insert(r.target_hash.clone());
                    out.push(json!({"seq": r.seq, "type": "redact", "by": r.subject_id.as_str(), "reason": r.reason, "target": r.target_hash}));
                }
                Record::Mark(m) => {
                    marked_hashes.insert(m.target_hash.clone());
                    out.push(json!({"seq": m.seq, "type": "mark", "by": m.subject_id.as_str(), "reason": m.reason, "target": m.target_hash}));
                }
                Record::Unmark(u) => {
                    marked_hashes.remove(&u.target_hash);
                    out.push(json!({"seq": u.seq, "type": "unmark", "by": u.subject_id.as_str(), "reason": u.reason, "target": u.target_hash}));
                }
                Record::Message(m) => {
                    let hash = rec.hash().unwrap_or_default();
                    let hidden = redacted_hashes.contains(&hash);
                    let marked = marked_hashes.contains(&hash);
                    let text = if hidden { "[redacted]".to_string() } else { m.text.clone() };
                    out.push(json!({
                        "seq": m.seq,
                        "type": "message",
                        "kind": m.kind,
                        "author": m.author_id.as_str(),
                        "text": text,
                        "redacted": hidden,
                        "marked": marked,
                        "ts": m.ts
                    }));
                }
                Record::Join(j) => {
                    out.push(json!({"seq": j.seq, "type": "join", "subject": j.subject_id.as_str(), "tier": j.tier, "is_ai": j.is_ai}));
                }
                Record::Leave(l) => {
                    out.push(json!({"seq": l.seq, "type": "leave", "subject": l.subject_id.as_str()}));
                }
                Record::HumanOverride(o) => {
                    out.push(json!({"seq": o.seq, "type": "human_override", "by": o.overridden_by.as_str(), "overrides": o.overrides_hash, "action": o.action, "reason": o.reason}));
                }
                Record::Refusal(r) => {
                    out.push(json!({"seq": r.seq, "type": "refused", "requester": r.requester_id.as_str(), "reason": r.reason}));
                }
                Record::RoomCreated(_) => {
                    out.push(json!({"seq": 0, "type": "created"}));
                }
                Record::PortLanding(_) | Record::Bookmark(_) | Record::Presence(_) => {}
            }
        }
        out
    }
}

File: chat-world/src/verify.rs
(Updated to include verification arms for Mark and Unmark records)
use crate::did::DidKey;
use crate::error::{Error, Result};
use crate::hash;
use crate::record::*;
use crate::room::{Member, Room};
use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};

/// Full offline verification: chain, signatures, and policy replay.
pub fn verify_room(room: &Room) -> Result<()> {
    let world_id = room.world_id();
    let mut prev: Option<String> = None;
    let mut members: HashMap<DidKey, Member> = HashMap::new();
    let mut redacted: HashSet<String> = HashSet::new();
    let mut marked: HashSet<String> = HashSet::new();

    for (i, rec) in room.records().iter().enumerate() {
        match rec {
            Record::RoomCreated(_) => {
                if i != 0 {
                    return Err(Error::Chain(format!("record {i}: room_created not at start")));
                }
            }
            _ => {
                if rec.prev() != prev.as_ref() {
                    return Err(Error::Chain(format!("record {i}: prev mismatch")));
                }
            }
        }

        let body = hash::body_for_signing(rec)?;
        let proofs = rec.proof();

        // Room proof required for all records except Leave.
        if !matches!(rec, Record::Leave(_)) {
            let Some(rp) = proofs.find_by_method(world_id) else {
                return Err(Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing room proof")));
            };
            rp.verify(&body)?;
        }

        match rec {
            Record::RoomCreated(r) => {
                let creator_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&r.creator_id)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing creator proof")))?;
                creator_proof.verify(&body)?;
                members.insert(r.creator_id.clone(), Member { tier: "terminal".into(), is_ai: false });
            }
            Record::Message(m) => {
                let Some(mem) = members.get(&m.author_id) else {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: author not a member")));
                };
                if tier_rank(&mem.tier).unwrap_or(0) < tier_rank("communicate").unwrap() {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: author tier insufficient")));
                }
                let author_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&m.author_id)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing author proof")))?;
                author_proof.verify(&body)?;
            }
            Record::Join(j) => {
                let subject_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&j.subject_id)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing subject proof")))?;
                subject_proof.verify(&body)?;
                members.insert(j.subject_id.clone(), Member { tier: j.tier.clone(), is_ai: j.is_ai });
            }
            Record::Leave(l) => {
                let subject_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&l.subject_id)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing subject proof")))?;
                subject_proof.verify(&body)?;
                members.remove(&l.subject_id);
            }
            Record::Redact(r) => {
                let subject_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&r.subject_id)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing subject proof")))?;
                subject_proof.verify(&body)?;
                let Some(_mem) = members.get(&r.subject_id) else {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: redactor not a member")));
                };
                let target = room.records().iter()
                    .find(|x| x.hash().ok().as_deref() == Some(&r.target_hash));
                match target {
                    Some(Record::Message(_)) => {}
                    _ => return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: redact target not a message"))),
                }
                redacted.insert(r.target_hash.clone());
            }
            Record::Mark(m) => {
                let subject_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&m.subject_id)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing subject proof")))?;
                subject_proof.verify(&body)?;
                let Some(mem) = members.get(&m.subject_id) else {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: marker not a member")));
                };
                let is_mod = room.template().moderation == "moderator"
                    && tier_rank(&mem.tier).unwrap_or(0) >= tier_rank("control").unwrap();
                if !is_mod {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: marking not permitted")));
                }
                marked.insert(m.target_hash.clone());
            }
            Record::Unmark(u) => {
                let subject_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&u.subject_id)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing subject proof")))?;
                subject_proof.verify(&body)?;
                let Some(mem) = members.get(&u.subject_id) else {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: unmarker not a member")));
                };
                let is_mod = tier_rank(&mem.tier).unwrap_or(0) >= tier_rank("control").unwrap();
                if !is_mod {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: unmarking not permitted")));
                }
                marked.remove(&u.target_hash);
            }
            Record::HumanOverride(o) => {
                let overrider_proof = proofs.find_by_method(&o.overridden_by)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing overrider proof")))?;
                overrider_proof.verify(&body)?;
                let Some(mem) = members.get(&o.overridden_by) else {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: overrider not a member")));
                };
                if mem.is_ai {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: only humans can override")));
                }
                if tier_rank(&mem.tier).unwrap_or(0) < tier_rank("control").unwrap() {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!("record {i}: overrider tier insufficient")));
                }
            }
            Record::Refusal(r) => {
                let _ = r;
            }
            Record::PortLanding(p) => {
                if !room.template().personal {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!(
                        "record {i}: port_landing only valid in personal rooms"
                    )));
                }
                let room_proof = proofs.find_by_method(world_id).ok_or_else(|| {
                    Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing room proof"))
                })?;
                room_proof.verify(&body)?;
                crate::transcript::verify_transcript(&p.transcript)
                    .map_err(|e| Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: transcript: {e}")))?;
            }
            Record::Bookmark(b) => {
                if !room.template().personal {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!(
                        "record {i}: bookmark only valid in personal rooms"
                    )));
                }
                let room_proof = proofs.find_by_method(world_id).ok_or_else(|| {
                    Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing room proof"))
                })?;
                room_proof.verify(&body)?;
                let _ = b;
            }
            Record::Presence(p) => {
                if !room.template().personal {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!(
                        "record {i}: presence only valid in personal rooms"
                    )));
                }
                let room_proof = proofs.find_by_method(world_id).ok_or_else(|| {
                    Error::Verification(format!("record {i}: missing room proof"))
                })?;
                room_proof.verify(&body)?;
                if p.subject_id != *world_id {
                    return Err(Error::Policy(format!(
                        "record {i}: presence subject must be the room's key"
                    )));
                }
            }
        }

        prev = Some(rec.hash()?);
    }

    Ok(())
}

/// Chain integrity only — useful when signatures are known-good.
pub fn verify_chain(room: &Room) -> Result<()> {
    let mut prev: Option<String> = None;
    for (i, rec) in room.records().iter().enumerate() {
        match rec {
            Record::RoomCreated(_) if i != 0 => {
                return Err(Error::Chain("room_created not at start".into()));
            }
            Record::RoomCreated(_) => {}
            _ => {
                if rec.prev() != prev.as_ref() {
                    return Err(Error::Chain(format!("record {i}: prev mismatch")));
                }
            }
        }
        prev = Some(rec.hash()?);
    }
    Ok(())
}

(Appended with tests for marking, withdrawing a mark, and privacy redaction)
// [Existing tests preserved, plus new tests for Step 1]

#[test]
fn moderator_can_mark_and_withdraw() -> Result<()> {
    let mut room = make_room();
    room.template.moderation = "moderator".into();
    let bob = Keypair::generate();
    let moderator = Keypair::generate();
    room.join(&bob, "communicate", false)?;
    room.join(&moderator, "control", false)?;
    
    let msg = room.post(&bob, "controversial speech", "normal")?;
    let h = msg.hash()?;
    
    // Moderator marks the message
    let mark_rec = room.mark(&moderator, &h, "breaks room guidelines")?;
    match mark_rec {
        Record::Mark(_) => {}
        _ => panic!("expected mark record"),
    }
    assert!(room.is_marked(&h));
    
    // Check visible_log shows marked: true, but text is intact
    let log_view = room.visible_log();
    let msg_entry = log_view.iter().find(|v| v["type"] == "message" && v["text"] == "controversial speech").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(msg_entry["marked"], true);
    assert_eq!(msg_entry["text"], "controversial speech");

    // Moderator withdraws the mark
    let unmark_rec = room.unmark(&moderator, &h, "reconsidered")?;
    match unmark_rec {
        Record::Unmark(_) => {}
        _ => panic!("expected unmark record"),
    }
    assert!(!room.is_marked(&h));

    verify_room(&room)
}

#[test]
fn personal_redaction_removes_text() -> Result<()> {
    let mut room = make_room();
    let bob = Keypair::generate();
    room.join(&bob, "communicate", false)?;
    
    let msg = room.post(&bob, "my secret contact info", "normal")?;
    let h = msg.hash()?;
    
    // Redact for privacy
    room.redact(&bob, &h, "pii removal")?;
    assert!(room.is_redacted(&h));
    
    let log_view = room.visible_log();
    let msg_entry = log_view.iter().find(|v| v["type"] == "message").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(msg_entry["redacted"], true);
    assert_eq!(msg_entry["text"], "[redacted]");

    verify_room(&room)
}


::Looks good. Please continue: ➡️


In my previous turn, I made two omissions against the technical lead's strict rules in the refresh document: I did not fix the single-pass ordering bug in Room::visible_log (where a message appears before its Redact or Mark record in the log, causing a single forward pass to miss the redaction/mark when rendering the message), and I gave a partial snippet for tests/integration.rs instead of the whole file.
Before moving to Step 2, here is the complete, self-contained STEP 1 deliverable following every instruction in the brief:
 * Two-pass visible_log: Room::visible_log first collects all active redactions and marks across the log (applying Mark and Unmark in sequence), and then renders each entry so that a message at seq: 2 redacted or marked at seq: 3 properly renders with redacted: true, text: "[redacted]" or marked: true.
 * Separation of Redact vs. Mark:
   * redact is for personal/private information (pii / contact details): any member may request it, it takes effect immediately, and it is permanent in the reader's view (text: "[redacted]", redacted: true).
   * mark and unmark are for messages that break a room's law: a moderator (control tier or higher when moderation == "moderator") signs a Mark record with a reason; the message text stays intact in the log and in visible_log, but carries "marked": true and "mark_reason" so a reader's view can fold it away while still allowing anyone to open it. A moderator can withdraw the mark with unmark.
   * override_ai can also withdraw an AI moderator's Mark when a human with control tier passes action = "withdraw_mark" (or "unmark"), whereas Redact cannot be undone by a member or moderator.
 * Every changed file given whole: Below are the five files touched by Step 1 (src/record.rs, src/room.rs, src/verify.rs, src/bin/chat-demo.rs, and tests/integration.rs), each complete from first line to last.
